The
\mathds{Z}_{2}\times\mathds{Z}_{2}
-quantum double model has
16
types of anyons as listed in Tab.
2
. There are six Lagrangian algebras in
\text{Z}_{1}({\mathrm{Vec}}_{\mathds{Z}_{2}\times\mathds{Z}_{2}})

(H,\omega)
, where
H\leqslant\mathds{Z}_{2}\times\mathds{Z}_{2}
and
\omega\in H^{2}(H,\mathds{C}^{\times})
:

\displaystyle A_{s}=\mathds{1}\oplus m_{1}\oplus m_{2}\oplus m_{0},\quad A_{c}=\mathds{1}\oplus f_{12}\oplus f_{21}\oplus f_{00},

(118)

\displaystyle A_{d}=\mathds{1}\oplus e_{0}\oplus m_{0}\oplus f_{00},\quad A_{b}=\mathds{1}\oplus e_{1}\oplus e_{2}\oplus e_{0}.

Here,
A_{s}
and
A_{c}
correspond to the trivial and non-trivial elements of

A_{o}
,
A_{e}
, and
A_{d}
correspond to three
\mathds{Z}_{2}
subgroups with trivial 2-cohomology, and
A_{b}
corresponds to the trivial subgroup
\{(1,1)\}
.

VI.3
Four gapped boundaries of
{S}_{3}
quantum double

The data and conventions for the
S_{3}
group and the
S_{3}
quantum double model are provided in Appendix
D
.

There are four Lagrangian algebras in
\text{Z}_{1}({\mathrm{Vec}}_{S_{3}})
:

\displaystyle A_{1}=\mathds{1}\oplus a_{1}\oplus 2a_{2},\quad

\displaystyle A_{2}=\mathds{1}\oplus a_{1}\oplus 2b,

(121)

\displaystyle A_{3}=\mathds{1}\oplus a_{2}\oplus c,\quad

\displaystyle A_{4}=\mathds{1}\oplus b\oplus c.

For
A_{1}=\mathds{1}\oplus a_{1}\oplus 2a_{2}
, there are two independent condensable internal DOFs of
a_{2}
, which are
\ket{a_2;1}
and
\ket{a_2;2}
.

\displaystyle M_{11}^{a_{2}}=\hat{Z}^{e}(\hat{Y}^{e}+e^{i{2\pi}/{3}}\hat{Y}^{r}+e^{-i{2\pi}/{3}}\hat{Y}^{r^{2}}),

(122)

\displaystyle M_{22}^{a_{2}}=\hat{Z}^{e}(\hat{Y}^{e}+e^{-i{2\pi}/{3}}\hat{Y}^{r}+e^{i{2\pi}/{3}}\hat{Y}^{r^{2}}),

(123)

Using Eq. (
91
), it is easy to get boundary term:

\displaystyle\hat{A}_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{6}\hat{M}^{\mathds{1}}+\frac{1}{6}\hat{M}^{a_{1}}+\frac{2}{6}(\hat{M}^{a_{2}}_{11}+\hat{M}^{a_{2}}_{22})

(124)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hat{Z}^{e}\hat{Y}^{e}.

For
A_{2}=\mathds{1}\oplus a_{1}\oplus 2b
, there are two independent condensable internal DOFs of
b
, which are
\ket{b;1}
and
\ket{b;2}
.

\displaystyle M_{11}^{b}=\hat{Z}^{r}(\hat{Y}^{e}+\hat{Y}^{r}+\hat{Y}^{r^{2}}),

(125)

\displaystyle M_{22}^{b}=\hat{Z}^{r^{2}}(\hat{Y}^{e}+\hat{Y}^{r}+\hat{Y}^{r^{2}}),

(126)

Thus, we have

\displaystyle\hat{A}_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{6}\hat{M}^{\mathds{1}}+\frac{1}{6}\hat{M}^{a_{1}}+\frac{2}{6}(\hat{M}^{b}_{11}+\hat{M}^{b}_{22})

(127)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{3}\left[(\hat{Z}^{e}+\hat{Z}^{r}+\hat{Z}^{r^{2}})(\hat{Y}^{e}+\hat{Y}^{r}+\hat{Y}^{r^{2}})\right].

For
A_{3}=\mathds{1}\oplus a_{2}\oplus c
, condensable internal DOF of
a_{2}
is
\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(\ket{a_2;1}+\ket{a_2;2})
and condensable internal DOF of
c
is
\ket{c;1}
.

\displaystyle\hat{M}^{a_{2}}_{\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(1+2),\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(1+2)}=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{mn}\hat{M}^{a_{2}}_{mn}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\hat{Z}^{e}(2\hat{Y}^{e}-\hat{Y}^{r}-\hat{Y}^{r^{2}}+2\hat{Y}^{x}-\hat{Y}^{xr}-\hat{Y}^{xr^{2}}).

(128)

\hat{M}_{11}^{c}=\hat{Z}^{x}(\hat{Y}^{e}+\hat{Y}^{x}).

(129)

Thus:

\displaystyle\hat{A}_{3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{6}\hat{M}^{\mathds{1}}+\frac{2}{6}\hat{M}^{a_{2}}_{\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(1+2),\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(1+2)}+\frac{3}{6}\hat{M}^{c}_{11}

(130)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left[(\hat{Z}^{e}+\hat{Z}^{x})(\hat{Y}^{e}+\hat{Y}^{x})\right].

For
A_{4}=\mathds{1}\oplus b\oplus c
, condensable internal DOF of
b
is
\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(\ket{b;1}+\ket{b;2})
and condensable internal DOF of
c
is
\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}(\ket{c;1}+\ket{c;2}+\ket{c;3})
.

\displaystyle\hat{M}^{b}_{\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(1+2),\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(1+2)}=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{mn}\hat{M}^{b}_{mn}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}(\hat{Z}^{r}+\hat{Z}^{r^{2}})(\hat{Y}^{e}+\hat{Y}^{r}+\hat{Y}^{r^{2}}+\hat{Y}^{x}+\hat{Y}^{xr}+\hat{Y}^{xr^{2}}),

\displaystyle\hat{M}^{c}_{\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}(1+2+3),\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}(1+2+3)}=\frac{1}{3}\sum_{mn}\hat{M}^{c}_{mn}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{3}(\hat{Z}^{xr}+\hat{Z}^{xr^{2}})(\hat{Y}^{e}+\hat{Y}^{r}+\hat{Y}^{r^{2}}+\hat{Y}^{x}+\hat{Y}^{xr}+\hat{Y}^{xr^{2}}).

Thus:

\displaystyle\hat{A}_{4}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{6}\hat{M}^{\mathds{1}}+\frac{2}{6}\hat{M}^{b}_{\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(1+2),\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(1+2)}

(133)

\displaystyle+\frac{3}{6}\hat{M}^{c}_{\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}(1+2+3),\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}(1+2+3)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{6}\bigl[(\hat{Z}^{e}+\hat{Z}^{r}+\hat{Z}^{r^{2}}+\hat{Z}^{x}+\hat{Z}^{xr}+\hat{Z}^{xr^{2}})

\displaystyle(\hat{Y}^{e}+\hat{Y}^{r}+\hat{Y}^{r^{2}}+\hat{Y}^{x}+\hat{Y}^{xr}+\hat{Y}^{xr^{2}})\bigr]

The complete results of gapped boundaries terms of
S_{3}
quantum double are shown in Tab.
4
. It is evident that the four sets of boundary terms correspond to the four subgroups of the
S_{3}
group. Following an analysis analogous to that presented in §
VI.1
, it can be readily shown that the four sets of boundary terms derived herein are equivalent to those constructed in Refs.
Kitaev and Kong 2012
;
Beigi et al. 2010
.

:

Local Boundary Term

\displaystyle\hat{Z}^{e}\hat{Y}^{e}

\mathds{1}\oplus a_{2}\oplus c

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left[(\hat{Z}^{e}+\hat{Z}^{x})(\hat{Y}^{e}+\hat{Y}^{x})\right]

\mathds{1}\oplus b\oplus c

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \frac{1}{6}\bigl[&(\hat{Z}^{e}+\hat{Z}^{r}+\hat{Z}^{r^{2}}+\hat{Z}^{x}+\hat{Z}^{xr}+\hat{Z}^{xr^{2}})\\
&(\hat{Y}^{e}+\hat{Y}^{r}+\hat{Y}^{r^{2}}+\hat{Y}^{x}+\hat{Y}^{xr}+\hat{Y}^{xr^{2}})\bigr]\end{aligned}

VII
Summary and Outlook

In this work, we have developed a systematic framework for constructing all 1+1D gapped boundaries of Kitaev’s quantum double models based on the macroscopic categorical formulation of Lagrangian algebras.

The core results in Section
V
report three major achievements:

1.

We systematically derive the boundary interaction terms (Theorem
V.4
) by ensuring consistency between anyon-creating/probing processes and the axioms of Lagrangian algebras.

2.

In lattice geometry with a zig-zag boundary, all distinct gapped boundary phases constructed in this work are supported within the same effective Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{Zig-Zag}}_{\mathrm{bdy}}
.

3.

We provide a microscopic characterization of bulk-to-boundary anyon condensation dynamics via the action of ribbon operators.

This work paves the way for several promising extensions:

•

Boundary Phase Transitions:
The framework developed in this work provides a solid foundation for studying pure boundary phase transitions, offering insights into their underlying mechanisms and properties.

•

Topological Wick Rotation:
By dualizing the effective Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{Zig-Zag}}_{\mathrm{bdy}}
to an appropriate Hilbert space of a 1+1D chain, we can explore the microscopic correspondence between gapped boundaries of 2+1D topological orders and 1+1D gapped quantum phases with symmetries. This correspondence will facilitate a detailed investigation of topological Wick rotation.

•

Generalization to
C^{*}
-Hopf Algebra Quantum Doubles:
Given the broad applicability of Lagrangian algebras, our construction can be extended to describe gapped boundaries of more general
C^{*}
-Hopf algebra quantum double models.

Appendix A
Algebraic computations of ribbon operators

Proof of the orthogonality of probing operators acting on the same path:

\displaystyle\hat{P}^{[C_{1},R_{1}]}_{m_{1}p_{1}}\hat{P}^{[C_{2},R_{2}]}_{m_{2}p_{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{|C_{1}|d_{R_{1}}|C_{2}|d_{R_{2}}}{|G|^{2}}\sum_{z_{1},z_{2}}\delta_{p_{1}r_{C_{1}}p_{1}^{-1},p_{2}r_{C_{2}}p_{2}^{-1}}\bar{\rho}^{R_{1}}_{m_{1}m_{1}}(z_{1})\bar{\rho}^{R_{2}}_{m_{2}m_{2}}(z_{2}){\hat{F}}^{p_{1}r_{C_{1}}p_{1}^{-1},p_{1}z_{1}p_{1}^{-1}p_{2}z_{2}p_{2}^{-1}}

(134)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{|C_{1}|d_{R_{1}}|C_{2}|d_{R_{2}}}{|G|^{2}}\delta_{C_{1},C_{2}}\delta_{p_{1},p_{2}}\sum_{z_{1},z_{2}}\bar{\rho}^{R_{1}}_{m_{1}m_{1}}(z_{1})\bar{\rho}^{R_{2}}_{m_{2}m_{2}}(z_{2}){\hat{F}}^{p_{1}r_{C_{1}}p_{1}^{-1},p_{1}z_{1}z_{2}p_{1}^{-1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{|C_{1}|d_{R_{1}}|C_{2}|d_{R_{2}}}{|G|^{2}}\delta_{C_{1},C_{2}}\delta_{p_{1},p_{2}}\sum_{z_{1},z}\bar{\rho}^{R_{1}}_{m_{1}m_{1}}(z_{1})\bar{\rho}^{R_{2}}_{m_{2}m_{2}}(z_{1}^{-1}z){\hat{F}}^{p_{1}r_{C_{1}}p_{1}^{-1},p_{1}zp_{1}^{-1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{|C_{1}|d_{R_{1}}}{|G|}\delta_{C_{1},C_{2}}\delta_{p_{1},p_{2}}\delta_{R_{1}R_{2}}\delta_{m_{1}m_{2}}\sum_{z}\bar{\rho}^{R_{1}}_{m_{1}m_{1}}(z){\hat{F}}^{p_{1}r_{C_{1}}p_{1}^{-1},p_{1}zp_{1}^{-1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta_{C_{1},C_{2}}\delta_{R_{1}R_{2}}\delta_{p_{1},p_{2}}\delta_{m_{1}m_{2}}\hat{P}^{[C_{1},R_{1}]}_{m_{1}p_{1}}.

Proof of the normalization of probing operators acting on the same path:

\displaystyle\sum_{C,R,m,p}\hat{P}^{[C,R]}_{mp}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{C}\sum_{R}\frac{|C|d_{R}}{|G|}\sum_{p}\sum_{m}\sum_{z\in Z(r_{C})}\bar{\rho}^{R}_{mm}(z){\hat{F}}^{pr_{C}p^{-1},pzp^{-1}}

(135)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{C}\sum_{R}\frac{|C|d_{R}}{|G|}\sum_{p}\sum_{z\in Z(r_{C})}\bar{\chi}^{R}(z){\hat{F}}^{pr_{C}p^{-1},pzp^{-1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{C}\frac{|C|}{|G|}\sum_{p}\sum_{z\in Z(r_{C})}\sum_{R}d_{R}\bar{\chi}^{R}(z){\hat{F}}^{pr_{C}p^{-1},pzp^{-1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{C}\sum_{p}\sum_{z\in Z(r_{C})}\delta_{z,e}{\hat{F}}^{pr_{C}p^{-1},pzp^{-1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{C}\sum_{p}{\hat{F}}^{pr_{C}p^{-1},e}=\sum_{g\in G}{\hat{F}}^{g,e}=\mathrm{Id}.

Proof of the Eq. (
31
):

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|G|}\sum_{C,R}{\frac{|C|d_{R}}{|G|}\hat{M}^{[C,R]}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|G|^{2}}\sum_{C}\sum_{R}{|C|d_{R}}\sum_{p}\sum_{z\in Z(r_{C})}\chi^{R}(z)\hat{F}^{pzp^{-1},pr_{C}p^{-1}}

(136)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|G|^{2}}\sum_{C}\sum_{p}\sum_{z\in Z(r_{C})}|C|\sum_{R}d_{R}\chi^{R}(z)\hat{F}^{pzp^{-1},pr_{C}p^{-1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|G|^{2}}\sum_{C}\sum_{p}\sum_{z\in Z(r_{C})}|C|\delta_{e,z}|Z(r_{c})|\hat{F}^{pzp^{-1},pr_{C}p^{-1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|G|}\sum_{C}\sum_{p}\hat{F}^{e,pr_{C}p^{-1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|G|}\sum_{g\in G}\hat{F}^{e,g}=\left(\frac{1}{|G|}\sum_{g\in G}\hat{Z}^{g}\right)\hat{Y}^{e}.

Appendix B
Anyon basis on torus

Consider the set of states
\{|g,h\rangle,\forall g,h\in G\}
defined on the simplest lattice decomposition of the torus as in Eq. (
41
). Two different types of bases of it, labeled by
L_{1}
and
L_{2}
, respectively, can be defined as follows:

\displaystyle\left\{|C,R;nq,mp\rangle_{L_{1}}\equiv\sum_{z\in Z(r_{C})}\rho^{R}_{nm}(z)\ket{qzp^{-1},pr_Cp^{-1}}\right\},

\displaystyle\left\{\ket{C,R;mp,nq}_{L_{2}}\equiv\sum_{z\in Z(r_{C})}\bar{\rho}^{R}_{mn}(z)\ket{pr_Cp^{-1},pzq^{-1}}\right\}.

The labels
L_{1}
and
L_{2}
also denote two non-contractable loops on the torus as in Fig.
8
. The reason that we use the same labels here will be clear later in this section. The orthogonality between basis states in each set can be verified directly using the orthogonality of group representations. The number of states in each set is:

\displaystyle\sum_{C}\sum_{R}|C|^{2}d_{R}^{2}=\sum_{C}|C|^{2}|Z(r_{C})|=|G|(\sum_{C}|C|)=|G|^{2},

which is equal to the number of states in
\{|g,h\rangle\}
.

In the set of bases labeled by
L_{1}
, if we choose the trivial conjugate class
C_{e}=\{e\}
of
G
, and the trivial representation
1
of
Z(e)=G
, whose element and character are both
1
, we observe that
\ket{C_e,1}_{L_{1}}
is a ground state on the torus:

\displaystyle\ket{G.S.}_{L_{1}}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\ket{C_e,1}_{L_{1}}=\sum_{z\in G}\rho^{1}(z)\ket{z,e}

(140)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{z\in G}\chi^{1}(z)\ket{z,e}=\sum_{z\in G}\ket{z,e}.

Acting ribbon operator
\hat{F}^{g,h}(L_{1})
along the loop
L_{1}
on the ground state
\ket{G.S.}_{L_{1}}
, we obtain the state
\ket{g,h}
:

\displaystyle\hat{F}^{g,h}(L_{1})\ket{G.S.}_{L_{1}}

\displaystyle=

}}=\ket{g,h}.

This relationship sets up a one-to-one correspondence between
\hat{F}^{g,h}(L_{1})
and
\ket{g,h}
. Therefore, Eq. (
141
) establishes a linear isomorphism between two linear spaces:

\mathfrak{C}_{L_{1}}\simeq\left\{\ket{g,h}\right\}_{\mathrm{Torus}}.

(142)

As discussed in §
III.2
, the
\mathfrak{C}
-algebras defined on different paths are isomorphic. Thus, we can omit the subscript
L_{1}
of
\mathfrak{C}_{L_{1}}
, and the operator-state correspondence holds in general on any fixed path of the ribbon operators:

\mathfrak{C}\simeq\left\{\ket{g,h}\right\}_{\mathrm{Torus}}.

(143)

Acting
\hat{M}^{[C,R]}_{nq,mp}(L_{1})
on
\ket{G.S.}_{L_{1}}
, we obtain another basis state
\ket{C,R;nq,mp}_{L_{1}}
in the basis set labeled by
L_{1}
:

\displaystyle\hat{M}^{[C,R]}_{nq,mp}(L_{1})\ket{G.S.}_{L_{1}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{z\in Z(r_{C})}\rho^{R}_{nm}(z)\hat{F}^{qzp^{-1},pr_{C}p^{-1}}\sum_{z^{\prime}\in G}\ket{z',e}

\displaystyle=\sum_{z\in Z(r_{C})}\rho^{R}_{nm}(z)\ket{qzp^{-1},pr_Cp^{-1}}

\displaystyle=\ket{C,R;nq,mp}_{L_{1}}.

(144)

The operator
\hat{M}^{[C,R]}_{nq,mp}(L_{1})
creates a pair of anyon (with internal DOF
nq
) and its dual anyon (with internal DOF
mp
), and moves one of them around the loop
L_{1}
. Since in general
nq
and
mp
are not the same, these two anyons cannot fuse into the vacuum. Thus,
\ket{C,R;nq,mp}_{L_{1}}
is not a ground state. This relationship sets up a one-to-one correspondence between the anyon-creating operator
\hat{M}^{[C,R]}_{nq,mp}(L_{1})
and the basis states
\ket{C,R;nq,mp}_{L_{1}}
.

Acting
\hat{M}^{[C,R]}(L_{1})
, which is the trace of
\hat{M}^{[C,R]}_{nq,mp}(L_{1})
, on the
\ket{G.S.}_{L_{1}}
, a pair of anyons are created, and then annihilated after moving one of them around loop
L_{1}
. The system returns to the ground state:

\displaystyle\hat{M}^{[C,R]}(L_{1})\ket{G.S.}_{L_{1}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{p\in\{p\}^{C}}\sum_{z\in Z(r_{C})}\chi^{R}(z)\hat{F}^{pzp^{-1},pr_{C}p^{-1}}\sum_{z^{\prime}\in G}\ket{z',e}

\displaystyle=\sum_{p\in\{p\}^{C}}\sum_{z\in Z(r_{C})}\chi^{R}(z)\ket{pzp^{-1},pr_Cp^{-1}}

\displaystyle=\ket{C,R}_{L_{1}}.

(145)

Therefore, starting from one particular ground state
\ket{G.S.}_{L_{1}}
, all the other degenerate ground states, in the form
\ket{C,R}_{L_{1}}
, can be obtained through the action of
\hat{M}^{[C,R]}(L_{1})
along the loop
L_{1}
.

Similarly, in the basis set labeled by
L_{2}
, if we choose
C=C_{e}
and
R=1
, we could also obtain a ground state on the torus:

\ket{G.S.}_{L_{2}}=\ket{C_e,1}_{L_{2}}=\sum_{z\in G}\bar{\chi}^{1}(z)\ket{e,z}=\sum_{z\in G}\ket{e,z}.

(146)

Acting
\hat{M}^{[C,R]}_{nq,mp}(L_{2})
along the loop
L_{2}
on
\ket{G.S.}_{L_{2}}
, we obtain another basis state
\ket{C,R;mp,nq}_{L_{2}}
in the basis set labeled by
L_{2}
:

\displaystyle\hat{M}^{[C,R]}_{nq,mp}(L_{2})\ket{G.S.}_{L_{2}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{z\in Z(r_{C})}\rho^{R}_{nm}(z)\hat{F}^{qzp^{-1},pr_{C}p^{-1}}\sum_{z^{\prime}\in G}\ket{e,z'}

\displaystyle=\sum_{z\in Z(r_{C})}\rho^{R}_{nm}(z)\ket{pr_Cp^{-1},pz^{-1}q^{-1}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{z\in Z(r_{C})}\bar{\rho}^{R}_{mn}(z)\ket{pr_Cp^{-1},pzq^{-1}}

\displaystyle=\ket{C,R;mp,nq}_{L_{2}}.

(147)

Due to these intuitive pictures, we call the basis
|C,R;nq,mp\rangle_{L_{1}}
and
\ket{C,R;mp,nq}_{L_{2}}
as anyon basis.

Now, we consider the
S
-transiformation of anyon-creating operator
\hat{M}^{[C,R]}_{nq,mp}(L_{1})
as defined in Eq. (
37
), and act it on
\ket{G.S.}_{L_{1}}
:

\displaystyle\mathbf{S}\left[\hat{M}^{[C,R]}_{nq,mp}(L_{1})\right]\ket{G.S.}_{L_{1}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{z\in Z(r_{C})}\bar{\rho}^{R}_{mn}(z){\hat{F}}^{pr_{C}p^{-1},pzq^{-1}}\sum_{z^{\prime}\in G}\ket{z',e}

\displaystyle=\sum_{z\in Z(r_{C})}\bar{\rho}^{R}_{mn}(z)\ket{pr_Cp^{-1},pzq^{-1}}

\displaystyle=\ket{C,R;mp,nq}_{L_{2}}.

(148)

And taking the trace gives:

\displaystyle\mathbf{S}\left[\hat{M}^{[C,R]}(L_{1})\right]\ket{G.S.}_{L_{1}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{p\in\{p\}^{C}}\sum_{z\in Z(r_{C})}\bar{\chi}^{R}(z)\hat{F}^{pr_{C}p^{-1},pz^{-1}p^{-1}}\sum_{z^{\prime}\in G}\ket{z',e}

\displaystyle=\sum_{p\in\{p\}^{C}}\sum_{z\in Z(r_{C})}\bar{\chi}^{R}(z)\ket{pr_Cp^{-1}, pz^{-1}p^{-1}}

\displaystyle=\ket{C,R}_{L_{2}}.

(149)

We also introduce a unitary operator
\hat{S}
acting on the torus states as:

\hat{S}\ket{g,h}\equiv\ket{h,g^{-1}},

(150)

\vbox{\hbox{\hbox to58.53pt{\vbox to58.53pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip-102.25pt\lower-109.7pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}

}}.

The operator
\hat{S}
relates the basis states with label
L_{1}
to that with label
L_{2}
:

\hat{S}\ket{C,R;nq,mp}_{L_{1}}=\ket{C,R;mp,nq}_{L_{2}}.

(151)

Therefore, we obtain the relation:

\displaystyle\hat{S}\left(\hat{M}^{[C,R]}_{nq,mp}(L_{1})\ket{G.S.}_{L_{1}}\right)=\mathbf{S}\left[\hat{M}^{[C,R]}_{nq,mp}(L_{1})\right]\ket{G.S.}_{L_{1}}

This relationship sets up the correspondence between the
S
-transformation of ribbon operators and the unitary operator
\hat{S}
on the torus states.

Appendix C
S-matrix of Kitaev’s quantum double

For Kitaev’s quantum double model, the following expressions for S-matrix elements can be extracted from
Dijkgraaf et al. 1991
or
Coste et al. 2000
.

\displaystyle S_{[CR],[C^{\prime}R^{\prime}]}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|G|}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in\{a\}^{C}\\
b\in\{b\}^{C^{\prime}}\end{subarray}}\delta\Big(ar_{C}a^{-1}br_{C^{\prime}}b^{-1},\ br_{C^{\prime}}b^{-1}ar_{C}a^{-1}\Big)

(153)

\displaystyle\times\bar{\chi}^{R}\big(a^{-1}br_{C^{\prime}}b^{-1}a\big)\bar{\chi}^{R^{\prime}}\big(b^{-1}ar_{C}a^{-1}b\big).

Noticed that for only
a^{-1}br_{C^{\prime}}b^{-1}a=z\in Z(r_{C})
, the
\delta
coefficient not equal to
0
. Then,
b^{-1}ar_{C}a^{-1}b
can be viewed as an element in
C^{Z(z)}_{r_{C}}
, which is the conjugate class of
r_{C}
in the subgroup
Z(z)
.

It was proved in
Coste et al. 2000
that the S-matrix can be expressed as:

\displaystyle S_{[CR],[C^{\prime}R^{\prime}]}=

\displaystyle\sum_{a\in\{a\}^{C}}\sum_{z\in Z(r_{C})}\delta(C^{\prime},C_{z})\bar{\chi}^{R}(z)\bar{\chi}^{R^{\prime}}(C^{Z(z)}_{r_{C}})

(154)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{|C|}{|G|}\sum_{z\in Z(r_{C})}\delta(C^{\prime},C_{z})\bar{\chi}^{R}(z)\bar{\chi}^{R^{\prime}}(C^{Z(z)}_{r_{C}}).

Two lemmas that we’re going to use are shown below.

Lemma C.1
.

Consider the definition of anyon-probe operator:

\displaystyle\hat{P}^{[C,R]}=\frac{d_{R}|C|}{|G|}\sum_{z\in Z(r_{C})}\sum_{q\in\{q\}^{C}}\bar{\chi}^{R}(z){\hat{F}}^{qr_{C}q^{-1},qzq^{-1}}

(155)

\displaystyle=\frac{d_{R}|C|}{|G|}\sum_{D}\bar{\chi}^{R}(D)\left[\sum_{q\in\{q\}^{C}}\sum_{d\in D}{\hat{F}}^{qr_{C}q^{-1},qdq^{-1}}\right].

Here
D
is a conjugate class in
Z(r_{C})
.

Given a group
H
, recall the column orthogonality of characters of representations:

\sum_{R}\bar{\chi}^{R}(K^{\prime}){\chi}^{R}(K)=\frac{|H|}{|K|}\delta_{K,K^{\prime}}.

(156)

This deduces:

\frac{|C||Z(r_{C})|}{|G||D|}\left[\sum_{q\in\{q\}^{C}}\sum_{d\in D}{\hat{F}}^{qr_{C}q^{-1},qdq^{-1}}\right]=\sum_{R}\frac{{\chi}^{R}(D)}{d_{R}}\hat{P}^{[C,R]}.

(157)

Lemma C.2
.

Using the disassembly of the group by the conjugate class and the centralizer, the following formula can be proved:

\sum_{q\in\{q\}^{C_{z}}}\sum_{d\in C^{Z(z)}_{r_{C}}}{\hat{F}}^{qdq^{-1},qzq^{-1}}=\frac{|C^{Z(z)}_{r_{C}}|}{|Z(z)|}\sum_{g\in G}{\hat{F}}^{gr_{C}g^{-1},gzg^{-1}}.

(158)

Similarly:

\sum_{p\in\{p\}^{C}}\sum_{k\in C^{Z(r_{C})}_{z}}{\hat{F}}^{qr_{C}q^{-1},qkq^{-1}}=\frac{|C^{Z(r_{C})}_{z}|}{|Z(r_{C})|}\sum_{g\in G}{\hat{F}}^{gr_{C}g^{-1},gzg^{-1}}.

(159)

These give:

\displaystyle\sum_{q\in\{q\}^{C_{z}}}\sum_{d\in C^{Z(z)}_{r_{C}}}{\hat{F}}^{qdq^{-1},qzq^{-1}}

(160)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{|C^{Z(z)}_{r_{C}}|}{|Z(z)|}\frac{|Z(r_{C})|}{|C^{Z(r_{C})}_{z}|}\sum_{p\in\{p\}^{C}}\sum_{k\in C^{Z(r_{C})}_{z}}{\hat{F}}^{qr_{C}q^{-1},qkq^{-1}}.

Now, we are ready to calculate:

\displaystyle|G|\sum_{[C^{\prime},R^{\prime}]}\bar{S}_{[C,R],[C^{\prime},R^{\prime}]}\frac{1}{|C^{\prime}|d_{R^{\prime}}}\hat{P}^{[C^{\prime},R^{\prime}]}

(161)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|G|\sum_{[C^{\prime},R^{\prime}]}\frac{|C|}{|G|}\sum_{z\in Z(r_{C})}\delta(C^{\prime},C_{z}){\chi}^{R}(z){\chi}^{R^{\prime}}(C^{Z(z)}_{r_{C}})\frac{1}{|C^{\prime}|d_{R^{\prime}}}\hat{P}^{[C^{\prime},R^{\prime}]}

(162)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|C|\sum_{R^{\prime}}\sum_{z\in Z(r_{C})}{\chi}^{R}(z){\chi}^{R^{\prime}}(C^{Z(z)}_{r_{C}})\frac{1}{|C_{z}|d_{R^{\prime}}}\hat{P}^{[C_{z},R^{\prime}]}

(163)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|C|\sum_{z\in Z(r_{C})}\frac{{\chi}^{R}(z)}{|C_{z}|}\sum_{R^{\prime}}\left[\frac{{\chi}^{R^{\prime}}(C^{Z(z)}_{r_{C}})}{d_{R^{\prime}}}\hat{P}^{[C_{z},R^{\prime}]}\right]

(164)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{lem1}}{=}

\displaystyle\frac{|C|}{|G|}\sum_{z\in Z(r_{C})}\frac{{\chi}^{R}(z)}{|C_{z}|}\frac{|C_{z}||Z(z)|}{|C^{Z(z)}_{r_{C}}|}\left[\sum_{p\in\{p\}^{C_{z}}}\sum_{d\in C^{Z(z)}_{r_{C}}}{\hat{F}}^{qr_{C}q^{-1},qdq^{-1}}\right]

(165)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{lem2}}{=}

\displaystyle\sum_{z\in Z(r_{C})}{{\chi}^{R}(z)}\frac{1}{|C^{Z(r_{C})}_{z}|}\sum_{q\in\{q\}^{C}}\sum_{k\in C^{Z(r_{C})}_{z}}{\hat{F}}^{qr_{C}q^{-1},qkq^{-1}}

(166)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{q\in\{q\}^{C}}\sum_{D}\sum_{d\in D}{{\chi}^{R}(r_{D})}\frac{1}{|C^{Z(r_{C})}_{r_{D}}|}\sum_{k\in C^{Z(r_{C})}_{r_{D}}}{\hat{F}}^{qr_{C}q^{-1},qkq^{-1}}

(167)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{q\in\{q\}^{C}}\sum_{D}{{\chi}^{R}(r_{D})}\sum_{k\in C^{Z(r_{C})}_{r_{D}}}{\hat{F}}^{qr_{C}q^{-1},qkq^{-1}}

(168)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{q\in\{q\}^{C}}\sum_{z\in Z(r_{C})}{{\chi}^{R}(z)}{\hat{F}}^{qr_{C}q^{-1},qzq^{-1}}.

(169)

This is exactly what we expected.

Appendix D

S_{3}
quantum double

S_{3}
group is generated by the elements
e
,
r
, and
x
. Here
e
is the identity element,
r^{3}=e
,
x^{2}=e
, and the relations
xr=r^{2}x
and
rx=xr^{2}
hold.

and
8
